## Abstract

We report drag-coefficient measurements on three smooth circular cylinders
(D = 25.4, 38.1 and 50.8 mm) towed vertically through a water tank at
0.2–1.1 m s⁻¹. The cylinders were surface-piercing with a 0.60 m submerged length,
which gives Reynolds numbers of 4.7 × 10³ to 5.0 × 10⁴ when the measured water
temperature is used to evaluate viscosity. Mean drag was measured with a single
calibrated load cell over a 5 s steady-towing window. After one run with load-cell
drift was excluded, the drag coefficient was nearly independent of diameter and
Reynolds number: C_D = 1.143 ± 0.033, 1.152 ± 0.026 and 1.157 ± 0.034
(mean ± s.d.) for the three diameters, and 1.151 ± 0.030 overall (29 runs).
The data overlap in Re across diameters. Over the full range they show a weak
upward trend of 0.058 ± 0.019 per decade of Re. We discuss the effects of
blockage (2.3–4.5 %), the free surface (Fr_D up to 2.2) and the finite
submerged length on the measured values. [TODO: one-sentence statement of the
contribution / why these data are useful.]

## 1 Introduction

[TODO: motivation. Why were these measurements made? E.g., a benchmark for the
facility, teaching-lab validation, a reference for a follow-on study.]

Flow past a circular cylinder in the subcritical regime (roughly 10³ < Re < 2 × 10⁵)
is a canonical bluff-body problem. A laminar boundary layer separates near
the shoulder of the cylinder, and the wake sheds vortices at a Strouhal number
close to 0.2 [CITE: Roshko 1961 JFM; Williamson 1996 Annu Rev Fluid Mech;
Zdravkovich 1997 *Flow Around Circular Cylinders* Vol. 1]. Classic data for
nominally two-dimensional cylinders put the sectional drag coefficient in this range
at roughly 1.0–1.2 [CITE: Wieselsberger 1921/1922; Achenbach 1968 JFM;
CHECK the exact range against the cited compilations].

Towing-tank measurements on practical cylinders depart from the 2-D ideal in three ways:

1. the cylinder has a finite length, with a free end near the tank floor;
2. it pierces a free surface that deforms at moderate Froude number; and
3. the tank walls and floor constrain the flow (blockage).

Finite length and a free end are generally reported to *reduce* C_D relative to the
2-D value [CITE: Zdravkovich 1997/2003; Hoerner 1965 *Fluid-Dynamic Drag*].
Blockage *increases* it [CITE: Maskell 1963 ARC R&M 3400; West & Apelt 1982 JFM].
The free surface adds wave-making effects that grow with Froude number
[CITE: Chaplin & Teigen 2003 JFM; CHECK relevance].

This paper presents a compact data set of 29 drag measurements on three
diameters spanning one decade in Re. The overlap in Re between diameters lets us
separate Reynolds-number from geometric (blockage, aspect-ratio, Froude) effects.
[TODO: state the specific aims / contributions.]

## 2 Experimental method

### 2.1 Facility and models

Tests were performed in the [TODO: facility name / institution] towing tank,
0.90 m wide and 0.75 m deep [TODO: length of tank, length of usable run,
carriage drive and speed control, how carriage speed was verified].

Three smooth circular cylinders were tested (Table 1):

- D = 25.4 mm (1 in)
- D = 38.1 mm (1.5 in)
- D = 50.8 mm (2 in)

[TODO: material, surface finish/roughness, how D was measured.] Each cylinder was
mounted vertically from the carriage and pierced the free surface. The submerged
length was L = 0.60 m [CHECK: L is central to C_D, so confirm it and how it was
measured; see note in Sect. 5.4]. This leaves a 0.15 m gap between the free
(lower) end and the tank floor [CHECK: confirm the lower end was free, with no
end plate]. The submerged aspect ratios are L/D = 23.6, 15.7 and 11.8.

**Table 1** Test matrix

| D (mm) | L/D | Blockage DL/(WH) | Date | Runs | U (m s⁻¹) | T_water (°C) | Re (× 10³) | Fr_D |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 25.4 | 23.6 | 0.023 | 2025-10-14 | 1–10 | 0.2–1.1 | 16.9–17.6 | 4.7–26.3 | 0.40–2.20 |
| 50.8 | 11.8 | 0.045 | 2025-11-03 | 11–20 | 0.2–1.1 | 15.0–15.6 | 8.9–49.9 | 0.28–1.56 |
| 38.1 | 15.7 | 0.034 | 2026-03-12 | 21–30 | 0.2–1.1 | 13.7–14.3 | 6.5–36.2 | 0.33–1.80 |

Re = UD/ν with ν evaluated at the water temperature logged for each run.
Fr_D = U/√(gD).

### 2.2 Force measurement and calibration

Streamwise force was measured with a single-axis load cell [TODO: make/model,
range, mounting arrangement, natural frequency of the mounted cylinder–balance
system]. The load cell was sampled at 200 Hz [TODO: DAQ hardware; any analog
filtering]. It was calibrated in situ by dead-weight loading (0–2000 g, ascending
and descending).

- **September 2025** (used for runs 1–20): a linear fit gave a sensitivity of
  0.02001 V N⁻¹ (standard error 0.05 %). The maximum residual was 0.32 mV
  (≈ 0.016 N), and the zero returned to within 0.03 mV after unloading.
- **March 2026** (used for runs 21–30): after the load cell was remounted and
  recalibrated, the sensitivity was 0.02129 V N⁻¹ (standard error 0.07 %). The
  maximum residual was 0.36 mV, and the zero returned to within 0.2 mV (≈ 0.01 N).

The 6 % change in sensitivity reflects the remounting [CHECK: confirm this is the
explanation]. Each calibration was applied only to the runs that followed it.

### 2.3 Procedure

For each run the cylinder was at rest for about 1.5 s. It was then accelerated
to the target speed (reached at t ≈ 2.5 s), towed at constant speed until
t ≈ 9 s, and decelerated to rest; recording continued to t = 12 s. Ten speeds
from 0.2 to 1.1 m s⁻¹ in 0.1 m s⁻¹ steps were run in ascending order for each
cylinder, one run per speed. [TODO: waiting time between runs for the tank to
settle; the log notes a residual wave from the previous run in run 4.] Water
temperature was recorded for every run.

### 2.4 Data reduction

For each run the load-cell voltage was tared using the mean over t < 1 s, before
the carriage moved. Force was obtained by dividing by the calibration sensitivity.
The mean drag F was taken over the steady-towing window 3 s < t < 8 s
(Fig. 1a). The drag coefficient was then computed as

  C_D = F / (½ ρ U² D L),

with ρ and ν evaluated at the measured water temperature
[CITE: source of water-property correlations, e.g. IAPWS; CHECK values].
U is the nominal carriage speed. No blockage, free-surface or end correction has
been applied to the values reported here; see Sect. 5.2.

Using the measured temperature instead of a nominal 20 °C matters for Re.
Because the water was 14–18 °C, the 20 °C viscosity would overestimate Re by
7–16 % (largest for the cold March tests). The effect on C_D through ρ is < 0.2 %.

**Exclusion of run 17.** The run log notes that the zero after run 17
(D = 50.8 mm, U = 0.8 m s⁻¹) "looked off". The record (Fig. 1b) shows that the
output drifted steadily throughout the run. Within the averaging window the
force rose from 12.4 to 14.4 N, whereas the neighbouring run 18 was flat to
within 0.03 N. After the carriage stopped, the signal sat 4.2 N above the
pre-run tare and was still rising. Run 17 would give C_D = 1.36, 17 % above the
mean of the other runs on that cylinder. It is excluded from all results.
[CHECK: is there a physical explanation, e.g. a cable snag or water ingress?
Was the load cell checked before run 18? Consider repeating this point.]
No other run showed a post-run offset larger than 0.003 N.

### 2.5 Uncertainty

[TODO: full uncertainty analysis. The following ingredients are available or
needed:]

- **Calibration sensitivity:** ≤ 0.07 % (standard error of fit).
- **Mean-force statistics:** the force fluctuates with an r.m.s. of 4.4–6.6 % of
  the mean, dominated by a periodic component. The 5 s window spans 15–90 cycles,
  so the random error of the window mean is well below 1 % [CHECK: quantify with
  an autocorrelation-based estimate].
- **Carriage speed:** C_D ∝ U⁻², so a 1 % speed error gives 2 % in C_D.
  [TODO: speed accuracy — this is likely the dominant term.]
- **Submerged length and diameter:** C_D ∝ 1/L and 1/D. [TODO: tolerances; note
  that the free-surface level changes while towing.]
- **Repeatability:** each condition was run only once. The run-to-run scatter
  of C_D within each cylinder (s.d. 0.026–0.034, i.e. about 2.5 %) is therefore the
  only available repeatability estimate. [TODO: repeat runs strongly recommended
  before submission.]

## 3 Results

### 3.1 Force records

Figure 1a shows a typical force record. After start-up the drag reaches a plateau
by t ≈ 2.5 s. It then oscillates about a steady mean until the carriage
decelerates at t ≈ 9 s, after which the force returns to zero within the noise.
Means taken over successive 1 s intervals within the 3–8 s window agree to
within about 1 % for every retained run. This indicates no residual start-up
transient or drift in the window.

![Fig. 1](figures/fig1_force_traces.png)

**Fig. 1** Load-cell force records. (a) Run 5 (D = 25.4 mm, U = 0.6 m s⁻¹),
typical of all retained runs. (b) Run 17 (excluded; drifting zero) compared with
run 18 on the same cylinder at the next speed. The shaded band marks the
3–8 s averaging window.

### 3.2 Drag coefficient

Figure 2 shows C_D against Re for all three cylinders; Table 2 summarises the
results, and per-run values are listed in Appendix A.

![Fig. 2](figures/fig2_cd_vs_re.png)

**Fig. 2** Drag coefficient versus Reynolds number (ν evaluated at measured
water temperature). Open symbol: run 17, excluded (Sect. 2.4).

**Table 2** Mean drag coefficient by cylinder (run 17 excluded)

| D (mm) | n | Re range (× 10³) | C_D mean | C_D s.d. | C_D min–max |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 25.4 | 10 | 4.7–26.3 | 1.143 | 0.033 | 1.093–1.181 |
| 38.1 | 10 | 6.5–36.2 | 1.152 | 0.026 | 1.103–1.192 |
| 50.8 | 9 | 8.9–49.9 | 1.157 | 0.034 | 1.107–1.216 |
| All | 29 | 4.7–49.9 | 1.151 | 0.030 | 1.093–1.216 |

The three diameters give mean C_D values within 0.014 of each other, smaller
than the within-cylinder scatter. Where the diameters overlap in Re
(≈ 9 × 10³ – 3 × 10⁴), their values cannot be distinguished. A least-squares
fit to all 29 points gives a weak increase of C_D with Re: dC_D/d(log₁₀ Re) =
0.058 ± 0.019 (standard error). This trend is driven largely by the lowest-Re
points on the two smaller cylinders. The 50.8 mm cylinder alone shows no trend
(slope −0.006).

### 3.3 Force fluctuations

The fluctuating component in every run is dominated by a single frequency f,
which scales with U/D. [CHECK — see note to authors: in this data set
fD/U = 0.400 for all 30 runs, to three decimal places. If the dominant drag
fluctuation is at twice the shedding frequency, as expected, this implies a
shedding Strouhal number of exactly 0.200 with no scatter across diameters,
speeds or test days. That is unusually exact for measured data and should be
verified before it is reported.]

## 4 Discussion

### 4.1 Comparison with two-dimensional data

The measured C_D ≈ 1.15 lies within or near the upper end of the range for
nominally 2-D cylinders at these Reynolds numbers [CITE + CHECK numerical
comparison]. This is notable because the finite aspect ratio (L/D = 12–24) and
free lower end would be expected to *lower* C_D below the 2-D value
[CITE: Zdravkovich; quantify the expected reduction for these L/D]. Two
effects act in the opposite direction:

1. **Blockage**, which increases the effective dynamic pressure; and
2. **free-surface wave drag at the higher Froude numbers**, which adds a
   pressure-drag component not present in deeply submerged flow.

### 4.2 Blockage

The solid blockage ratio DL/(WH) is 2.3, 3.4 and 4.5 % for the three cylinders.
For a bluff body with C_D ≈ 1.15, standard wake-blockage corrections reduce C_D by
an amount of the same order as the blockage ratio times C_D. That is roughly
2–5 %, and larger for the larger cylinder [TODO: apply a specific correction,
e.g. Maskell 1963 or the cylinder-specific results of West & Apelt 1982, and
report corrected values alongside uncorrected ones; CHECK applicability to a
surface-piercing, finite-length body].

If blockage were the main geometric effect, the uncorrected C_D would rise
systematically with D. The measured means do increase slightly with D
(1.143 → 1.152 → 1.157), but by less than the scatter. A blockage correction
therefore matters for comparison with 2-D data but does not change the
conclusion that the three diameters agree.

### 4.3 Free-surface and aspect-ratio effects

The diameter-based Froude number reaches 2.2 for the smallest cylinder at the
highest speed, and the three cylinders reach the same Re at different Fr_D. If
free-surface effects were significant, C_D at fixed Re would differ between
diameters. Within the scatter we see no such difference. A joint regression on
log Re and Fr_D gives a negligible Fr_D coefficient (0.009 per unit Fr_D).
[CHECK: the data set is small and Re and Fr_D are correlated within each
cylinder, so state this conclusion cautiously.]

Similarly, L/D varies by a factor of two between cylinders without a resolvable
change in C_D. [TODO: compare with expected aspect-ratio dependence.]

### 4.4 Data-reduction note

[CHECK: an early analysis (notes, 2025-10-20) gave C_D ≈ 0.98 for the 25.4 mm
cylinder and questioned L. The current value is 1.14, and the ratio 1.14/0.98
≈ 1.16 is close to 0.70/0.60. This suggests that the change came from correcting
L, but the folder does not record it. Confirm that L = 0.60 m is the correct
submerged length, because it scales every C_D directly.]

## 5 Conclusions

[Draft; revise once the TODO/CHECK items are resolved.]

1. Drag on three smooth surface-piercing circular cylinders (D = 25.4–50.8 mm,
   L = 0.60 m) was measured over 4.7 × 10³ < Re < 5.0 × 10⁴ in a towing tank.
2. Without blockage, free-surface or end corrections, C_D = 1.151 ± 0.030
   (29 runs). The per-cylinder means differ by less than the run-to-run scatter.
3. The data show only a weak Re dependence (0.058 ± 0.019 per decade). There is
   no resolvable dependence on diameter, Froude number (up to 2.2) or aspect
   ratio (12–24) beyond the scatter.
4. One run was excluded because of load-cell drift, identified from a non-zero
   post-run offset. We recommend checking the post-run zero routinely as a
   quality criterion.

=== ./paper/make_figures.py
# Figures and summary numbers for the paper draft. Run from the project root:
#   python paper/make_figures.py
# Uses processed/forces*.csv (reproduced from raw data) + data/run_log.csv.
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import matplotlib
matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import matplotlib.ticker

L, W, H = 0.60, 0.90, 0.75  # submerged length, tank width, tank depth (m)
EXCLUDE = [17]  # load-cell drift during run (see paper, Sect. 2.4)

INK, INK2, GRID = "#0b0b0b", "#52514e", "#e4e3df"
SERIES = {"1in": ("#2a78d6", "o"), "1.5in": ("#eb6834", "s"), "2in": ("#1baf7a", "^")}
LABEL = {"1in": "D = 25.4 mm", "1.5in": "D = 38.1 mm", "2in": "D = 50.8 mm"}

plt.rcParams.update({
    "font.size": 9, "axes.edgecolor": INK2, "axes.labelcolor": INK,
    "xtick.color": INK2, "ytick.color": INK2, "axes.spines.top": False,
    "axes.spines.right": False, "lines.linewidth": 1.0, "savefig.dpi": 300,
})


def water_props(T):
    """Density and kinematic viscosity of pure water at T (deg C)."""
    rho = 1000 * (1 - (T + 288.9414) / (508929.2 * (T + 68.12963)) * (T - 3.9863) ** 2)
    mu = 2.414e-5 * 10 ** (247.8 / (T + 273.15 - 140))
    return rho, mu / rho


f = pd.concat([pd.read_csv("processed/forces.csv"), pd.read_csv("processed/forces_b3.csv")])
df = f.merge(pd.read_csv("data/run_log.csv"), left_on="run", right_on="run_id")
D = df.diameter_mm / 1000
df["rho"], df["nu"] = water_props(df.water_temp_c)
df["Re"] = df.speed_m_s * D / df.nu
df["Cd"] = df.F_mean / (0.5 * df.rho * df.speed_m_s**2 * D * L)
df["Fr_D"] = df.speed_m_s / np.sqrt(9.81 * D)
df["blockage"] = D * L / (W * H)
df["excluded"] = df.run.isin(EXCLUDE)
df.round(5).to_csv("paper/results_table.csv", index=False)

good = df[~df.excluded]
summ = good.groupby("diameter_mm").agg(
    n=("Cd", "size"), Cd_mean=("Cd", "mean"), Cd_std=("Cd", "std"),
    Re_min=("Re", "min"), Re_max=("Re", "max"), Fr_max=("Fr_D", "max"),
    blockage=("blockage", "first"))
print(summ.round(4))
print("all:", round(good.Cd.mean(), 3), "+/-", round(good.Cd.std(), 3), "n =", len(good))

# Fig. 1: example force trace (run 5) and the drifting run 17 vs neighbour run 18
fig, axs = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(7, 2.6), sharex=True)
for ax, runs, sens, title in [
    (axs[0], [(5, "2025-10-14")], 0.0200, "(a) Run 5: D = 25.4 mm, U = 0.6 m/s"),
    (axs[1], [(18, "2025-11-03"), (17, "2025-11-03")], 0.0200, "(b) Runs 17 and 18: D = 50.8 mm"),
]:
    for run, day in runs:
        d = pd.read_csv(f"data/raw/{day}/run_{run:03d}.csv", comment="#")
        F = (d.loadcell_V - d.loadcell_V[d.time_s < 1].mean()) / sens
        color = "#e34948" if run == 17 else "#2a78d6"
        ax.plot(d.time_s, F, lw=0.4, color=color)
        if len(runs) > 1:
            ax.annotate(f"run {run}", (10.2, F[d.time_s > 10.5].mean()),
                        xytext=(0, 6), textcoords="offset points", color=INK2, fontsize=8)
    ax.axvspan(3, 8, color=GRID, zorder=0, lw=0)
    ax.axhline(0, color=INK2, lw=0.5)
    ax.set_title(title, fontsize=9, loc="left", color=INK)
    ax.set_xlabel("$t$ (s)")
axs[0].set_ylabel("$F$ (N)")
fig.tight_layout()
fig.savefig("paper/figures/fig1_force_traces.png")
fig.savefig("paper/figures/fig1_force_traces.pdf")

# Fig. 2: Cd vs Re
fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(4.5, 3.2))
ax.grid(True, which="major", color=GRID, lw=0.5)
for cyl in ["1in", "1.5in", "2in"]:
    g = df[df.cylinder == cyl]
    c, m = SERIES[cyl]
    gg = g[~g.excluded]
    ax.plot(gg.Re, gg.Cd, m, ms=6 if m == "^" else 5, color=c, mec="white", mew=0.6, label=LABEL[cyl])
    gx = g[g.excluded]
    if len(gx):
        ax.plot(gx.Re, gx.Cd, m, ms=5, mfc="none", mec=c, mew=1.0)
        ax.annotate("run 17 (excluded)", (gx.Re.iloc[0], gx.Cd.iloc[0]), xytext=(6, -3),
                    textcoords="offset points", fontsize=7.5, color=INK2)
ax.set_xscale("log")
ax.set_xlim(4e3, 6e4)
ax.set_xticks([5e3, 1e4, 2e4, 5e4], ["5000", "10 000", "20 000", "50 000"])
ax.xaxis.set_minor_formatter(matplotlib.ticker.NullFormatter())
ax.set_xlabel("$Re = UD/\\nu$")
ax.set_ylabel("$C_D$")
ax.set_ylim(1.0, 1.4)
ax.legend(frameon=False, fontsize=8, loc="upper left")
fig.tight_layout()
fig.savefig("paper/figures/fig2_cd_vs_re.png")
fig.savefig("paper/figures/fig2_cd_vs_re.pdf")

=== ./old_matlab/towtank_proc.m
% towtank_proc.m  - J. Rivera, 2024
% mean drag from one run file. usage: F = towtank_proc('run_001.csv', 0.02)
function F = towtank_proc(fname, sens)
    d = readmatrix(fname, 'NumHeaderLines', 3);
    t = d(:,1); v = d(:,2);
    tare = mean(v(t < 1));
    f = (v - tare) / sens;
    F = mean(f(t > 3 & t < 8));
end

=== ./README.md
# towtank-drag

Drag on smooth circular cylinders towed through the tank. 1in, 2in and
1.5in cylinders, 0.6 m submerged.

## Processing

1. `python process.py` (change DATA path to wherever the raw data is)
2. `python process_b3.py` for the March data (new DAQ format)
3. run `analysis.ipynb` -> Cd vs Re plot in figures/

`plot_timeseries.py` makes the example trace figure.

Calibration files are in data/calibration. Run log is data/run_log.csv.

old_matlab/ is Jamie's original processing script, kept for reference.

=== ./plot_timeseries.py
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import pandas as pd

d = pd.read_csv("data/raw/2025-10-14/run_005.csv", comment="#")
sens = 0.0200
F = (d.loadcell_V - d.loadcell_V[d.time_s < 1].mean()) / sens

plt.figure(figsize=(6, 3))
plt.plot(d.time_s, F, lw=0.5)
plt.axvspan(3, 8, color="gray", alpha=0.2)
plt.xlabel("t (s)")
plt.ylabel("F (N)")
plt.title("run 5")
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig("figures/timeseries_run005.png", dpi=150)
=== ./process.py
# process tow tank runs -> mean drag force for each run
import glob
import os

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

DATA = "/Users/mkim/Dropbox/towtank/data/raw"
BATCHES = ["2025-10-14", "2025-11-03"]

cal = pd.read_csv("data/calibration/loadcell_2025-09.csv")
sens = np.polyfit(cal.mass_g / 1000 * 9.81, cal.voltage_V, 1)[0]  # V/N
print("sensitivity", sens)

rows = []
for b in BATCHES:
    for f in sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(DATA, b, "run_*.csv"))):
        d = pd.read_csv(f, comment="#")
        tare = d.loadcell_V[d.time_s < 1.0].mean()
        F = (d.loadcell_V - tare) / sens
        steady = (d.time_s > 3) & (d.time_s < 8)
        rows.append({
            "run": int(os.path.basename(f)[4:7]),
            "F_mean": F[steady].mean(),
            "F_std": F[steady].std(),
        })

out = pd.DataFrame(rows)
out.to_csv("processed/forces.csv", index=False)
print("done", len(out))
=== ./process_b3.py
# same as process.py but for the march runs (new DAQ software, ; separated)
# TODO merge into process.py
import glob
import os

import pandas as pd

SENS = 0.0213  # V/N, from march cal

rows = []
for f in sorted(glob.glob("data/raw/2026-03-12/run_*.csv")):
    d = pd.read_csv(f, sep=";")
    tare = d.V[d.t < 1.0].mean()
    F = (d.V - tare) / SENS
    steady = (d.t > 3) & (d.t < 8)
    rows.append({
        "run": int(os.path.basename(f)[4:7]),
        "F_mean": F[steady].mean(),
        "F_std": F[steady].std(),
    })

pd.DataFrame(rows).to_csv("processed/forces_b3.csv", index=False)
